It feels aimed at me. It feels like it’s pushing me, wearing me down, trying to move me out because I’m one of the old hands there.\n\nSo if they said “glitch,” I’d want to know why the glitch keeps lining up around me. If they said “efficiency protocol,” I’d want to know why that protocol seems to land on me the way it does. Maybe it would make me quiet down for a little while, maybe I’d try to accept it, but I don’t think it would really change what I believe unless it explained all of it. Because right now, to me, it’s not just a computer doing computer things. It’s personal.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of time, even if just a day or two, where you didn't feel like the computer system was targeting you?\n\nPatient: A whole day or two? No… not really, not once it started feeling this way.\n\nI mean, there might be moments when I’m away from the warehouse, or when I’m busy with something else, where I’m not actively thinking about it every second. Like if I’m driving home or trying to get through dinner, I can sometimes push it to the side for a little bit. But that’s different from not believing it. It’s still there in the back of my mind, because I know what’s been happening.\n\nWhen I’m back on the floor, with the scanner in my hand, it comes right back clear as day. The routing system isn’t just assigning work like a normal computer anymore. It’s been marking my moves — where I am, what I’m doing — and then the scanner messages come through in a way that feels aimed at me. Not random. Not just business as usual. It feels like it’s steering me and wearing me down, trying to push me out because I’m one of the old hands there.\n\nSo no, I don’t think I’ve had a stretch where I truly felt, “Oh, it’s not targeting me.” Maybe I’ve had short breaks where I wasn’t staring straight at it, but I haven’t stopped knowing it’s happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever woken up in the morning feeling like the computer system is not targeting you?\n\nPatient: No… I can’t say I’ve woken up and felt like, “Oh, it’s not happening.” Not once it got into my head the way it has.\n\nSome mornings I wake up and for a few seconds I’m just thinking about getting my coffee, getting dressed, my back hurting, whatever normal thing is there first. But then it comes back in. I remember I have to go back into that warehouse, and that routing system is waiting there, and the scanner is going to start up again with those messages. It’s not like I wake up with a clean slate and think it’s just a regular computer.\n\nI know how it sounds when I say it out loud, but to me it isn’t just a machine assigning routes anymore. It’s been marking where I go, what I’m doing, how I’m moving around the floor, and then the scanner messages come through in a way that feels directed at me. Like it’s trying to steer me into mistakes or wear me down until I leave, because I’ve been there a long time. I’m one of the old hands there.\n\nSo mornings might be quieter for a minute, before I’m actually at work, but the belief doesn’t go away. I still know it’s targeting me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a specific person behind the computer system that's targeting you?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I can put a name to a person, like say, “It’s him” or “It’s her.” I’m not saying I’ve seen somebody sitting at a keyboard with my name on the screen or anything like that. But I have a hard time believing it’s just the computer doing it all by itself.\n\nThe routing system at the warehouse… it’s not just a computer to me anymore. It’s been marking my moves — where I am, what I’m doing, how I’m moving around — and then the scanner messages come through in a way that feels like they’re meant for me. Like it’s pushing me, steering me, setting me up to look bad or wear me down. And I keep coming back to the same thing: I’ve been there a long time. I’m one of the old hands there. Maybe somebody wants me gone and they’re using the system to do it.\n\nSo no, I don’t have one specific person I can point to. But it feels like there’s intent behind it. It feels too directed to be random.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the computer system or whoever might be behind it is only targeting you while you're at work?\n\nPatient: Mostly it happens through work, yes… I mean, that’s where the routing system is. That’s where the scanners are. That’s where it can mark where I am and what I’m doing and send those messages through, the ones that feel like they’re meant to push me around or make me mess up.\n\nBut I wouldn’t say it’s only at work in the sense that I just clock out and it disappears. When I’m home, the scanner isn’t in my hand, so it’s not happening in the same direct way. But I still know what it’s doing. I still think about how it’s waiting there, how it’s been building this picture of my movements and steering things against me. It kind of comes home with me in my head, if that makes sense.\n\nThe actual targeting, the way I experience it most clearly, is on the warehouse floor. That’s where it has access to me. That’s where it sends the messages and routes me a certain way, and it feels like it’s trying to wear me down because I’ve been there so long. Like I’m one of the old hands and it wants me out.\n\nSo… not exactly only at work, but work is where it’s active. Home is more where I’m thinking about what it’s already doing and what it’s going to do when I go back.\n\nDoctor: Can you think of any specific incident or event at work that first made you feel like the computer system was targeting you?\n\nPatient: I can think of the shift where it really clicked for me, yes. I don’t know if I can give you the exact day, but it was at the warehouse, with the routing system and the handheld scanner. I’d been there long enough to know what normal glitches look like, what a bad assignment looks like, what random confusion looks like. This didn’t feel like that.\n\nIt started with the way the routes were coming through. It kept sending me in these patterns that didn’t make sense, like back and forth, out of order, making me look slow or like I wasn’t keeping up. And then the scanner messages — the little prompts and corrections — they started feeling too pointed. Not just “go here” or “scan this,” but like the timing of them was aimed at me, like it was watching how I moved and then pushing me into the next mistake.\n\nThat was the first time I remember thinking, this isn’t just a computer assigning work. It’s marking where I go. It knows my moves. And it’s using those scanner messages to push me, to wear me down, like it wants me out because I’m one of the old hands there.\n\nI know someone else might say it was just the system being inefficient, but I’ve worked around that kind of equipment a long time. This felt different. It felt directed at me.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period of more than a week where you didn't feel like the computer system was targeting you?\n\nPatient: No… not really. I mean, maybe there were days where I didn’t talk about it as much, or I tried to keep my head down and just do the job, but more than a week where I didn’t feel it was happening? No, I can’t say that.\n\nOnce I understood what the routing system was doing, it didn’t just go away. That’s part of why I’m here, because it isn’t just a computer to me anymore. It’s been marking my moves at the warehouse — where I go, how fast I’m moving, what aisles I’m sent to — and then the scanner messages come through in ways that feel like they’re pushing me, correcting me, setting me up. Like it’s trying to make me look bad or make me worn down enough to leave.\n\nSome shifts are worse than others, sure. There might be a day where it’s quieter, or I think, okay, maybe I can get through today without it coming down on me so hard. But even then I still know it’s there, still tracking and building off what I do. I’m one of the old hands there, and that’s what keeps sticking in my mind — that it’s pushing me out because I’ve been there so long.\n\nSo, no, I don’t think I’ve had a full week where I truly felt free of it. It’s been there in one way or another.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment where you completely stopped believing the computer system was targeting you?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I ever completely stopped believing it. I’ve had moments where I’ve tried to talk myself down, like, “Maybe it’s just the routing system being messed up,” or “Maybe everybody’s scanner is acting strange today.” I can say that, and I’ve tried to say that to myself because I don’t want to be walking around upset all the time.\n\nBut underneath that, no, I still know what it’s doing. That’s why I’m here, really. The routing system at the warehouse isn’t just a computer to me anymore. It’s been marking my moves — where I go, how it sends me from one place to another, how the scanner messages come in at just the right time to push me or make me look like I’m falling behind. It feels directed. It feels like it’s trying to push me out because I’m one of the old hands there.\n\nSo maybe there are minutes, or part of a shift, where I try not to focus on it as much. But completely stopped believing it? No. I don’t think that’s happened.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a moment, no matter how brief, where you didn't think the computer system was targeting you?\n\nPatient: Maybe for a few seconds at a time, if I’m being honest… like if I’m distracted by lifting something, or talking to somebody, or just trying to get through a task. But even then, it’s not like I really stop believing it. It’s more like I push it to the side because I have to keep working.\n\nI’m here because the routing system at the warehouse isn’t just a computer to me anymore. I know it’s been marking my moves — where it sends me, how it changes the route, how the scanner messages come through — and it feels like those messages are meant to push me out. Like they’re trying to wear me down because I’m one of the old hands there.\n\nSo if you mean, did I ever have a clear moment where I thought, “No, this isn’t happening, it’s just a normal system,” then no. Not really. I might try to tell myself that for a moment, but I don’t believe it deep down. It always comes back to the same thing: it knows what I’m doing, and it’s using the scanners and routing to push me.\n\nDoctor: Did anything significant change at work or in your personal life around the time you started feeling like the computer system was targeting you?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, there was a change. It didn’t come out of nowhere. It started after they changed the warehouse routing system, when they put in that newer way of sending us around, and around that same time they moved us onto rotating overnight shifts.\n\nThat’s when I started noticing it. I mean, not all in one single minute like a light switch, but that’s when it began to feel different. Before that I knew the place, I knew the routines, I knew how the work flowed. Then the system changed, the scanner started directing things differently, and with the overnights rotating around, everything felt off — like I couldn’t get my footing the same way.\n\nIt’s been going on about two years and three months now, if I’m counting right. I remember because it was tied to that change at work. The routing system came in, the shifts changed, and after that it just… it started feeling like the system wasn’t just assigning work anymore, like it had turned toward me somehow.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel like people or things outside of the warehouse, like on the street or at home, are watching you or trying to send you messages?\n\nPatient: Not really in the same way, no. I mean… I can get on edge outside of work because I’m already carrying it around in my head, you know? If I’ve had a bad shift, I might still be thinking about what the scanner did or why it sent me a certain way, and then I’m tense when I’m driving home or sitting at home. But the actual thing, the targeting, that’s the warehouse system.\n\nThat’s why I keep coming back to the routing computer and the scanners. It’s not just a machine assigning tasks anymore. It knows where I’m going, it marks my moves, it sends those scanner messages at just the right times, and it’s trying to push me out because I’ve been there a long time — I’m one of the old hands. That’s where the messages are coming through.\n\nSo on the street or at home… I don’t know, I wouldn’t say people out there are sending me messages. It’s more that the warehouse system stays with me after I leave because I know I have to go back to it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think anything or anyone else, besides the warehouse computer system, is trying to target or harm you?\n\nPatient: I don’t think it’s anyone else in the same way. I mean, I’m not saying every person is out to get me or something like that. It’s really centered on that system at work — the routing computer, the scanners, the way it sends me places and times things.\n\nThat’s what I keep trying to explain. It isn’t just a computer to me anymore. It’s been marking my movements, watching where I go in the warehouse, and those scanner messages… they don’t feel random. They come through in a way that feels directed at me, like it’s trying to wear me down or make me look bad so I’ll leave. Because I’ve been there a long time, I’m one of the old hands, and it feels like the system has turned on people like me.\n\nSo if you mean, do I think some other person outside of that is trying to harm me, no, not really. But the warehouse system itself — yes. That’s the thing targeting me.\n\nDoctor: Do you think you are being targeted or watched by anything or anyone outside of the warehouse computer system?\n\nPatient: No, not outside of that, not really. I don’t feel like people on the street or neighbors or anything like that are watching me. It’s the warehouse system. That’s why I keep saying it’s not just a regular computer to me anymore.\n\nThe routing system knows where I’m going, it marks my moves, and then the scanner messages come through at these times that feel… aimed at me. Like it’s trying to make me look slow, or send me in circles, or put me in situations where I’ll mess up. And I think it’s because I’ve been there a long time — I’m one of the old hands — and it wants to push me out.\n\nSo if you mean outside the warehouse computer system, no, I don’t think there’s some other person or thing doing it. It’s that system. That’s what’s targeting me.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're being targeted or watched by anything or anyone when you're not at work?\n\nPatient: When I’m not at work, I don’t really think it’s a separate person or something following me around, if that’s what you mean. It’s not like I’m walking down the street thinking every car or neighbor is part of it.\n\nBut… it does stay with me. Because I know I have to go back there. The routing system at the warehouse, that’s the thing. It’s not just a computer to me anymore. I know it’s been marking my moves, watching where I go in the building, and then sending messages through the scanners in these ways that feel like they’re meant for me. Like it’s trying to push me out because I’ve been there so long — I’m one of the old hands.\n\nSo at home, no, I wouldn’t say something new is watching me there. But I’m still thinking about what the system did, what it sent me, why it sent me there, and what it’s going to do next shift. It kind of follows me in my head, but the targeting itself is from the warehouse system."
